{"content": "1|#!/usr/bin/env python3\n2|\"\"\"\n3|Denaro - Dashboard server (:8913).\n4|\n5|Serve la dashboard HTML \"Denaro Neon Grid\" e fa da proxy verso l'Infra\n6|Aggregator (:8912), aggiungendo una last-good cache per non lasciare mai la\n7|pagina senza dati.\n8|\n9|Perche' esiste (incident 2026-09-10):\n10|  * la unit systemd denaro-dashboard-mc2.service puntava a\n11|    /home/sergio/denaro/serve_dashboard.py, file che NON esisteva piu':\n12|    al primo restart il servizio sarebbe andato in crash-loop con\n13|    \"can't open file ... [Errno 2]\" e la dashboard sarebbe rimasta nera;\n14|  * con HTTPServer mono-thread una singola richiesta lenta all'aggregator\n15|    bloccava tutta la dashboard;\n16|  * se l'aggregator era giu', /api/infra.json tornava un errore e la pagina\n17|    mostrava \"SIGNAL LOST\" anche se il dato buono di 30 secondi prima c'era.\n18|\n19|Cosa fa adesso:\n20|  GET /                 -> dashboard HTML (no-store)\n21|  GET /dashboard        -> idem\n22|  GET /dashboard/       -> idem (prima era 404)\n23|  GET /api/infra.json   -> JSON dell'aggregator (proxy) + fallback last-good\n24|  GET /infra.json       -> alias di /api/infra.json\n25|  GET /scommessa.json   -> dato della scommessa asimmetrica (spec DSH 19/09)\n26|  GET /api/dsh.json     -> stato del bridge DSH/Stella\n27|  GET /healthz          -> stato del servizio in JSON\n28|\n29|Configurazione (env var, override da riga di comando):\n30|  DASH_HOST         default 127.0.0.1\n31|  DASH_PORT         default 8913\n32|  DASH_HTML_DIR     default /home/sergio/denaro/denaro\n33|  DASH_HTML_FILE    default dashboard_infra.html\n34|  AGG_URL           default http://127.0.0.1:8912/infra.json\n35|  AGG_TIMEOUT       default 6 (secondi)\n36|  DASH_CACHE_FILE   default /home/sergio/denaro/health/infra_last_good.json\n37|  DASH_STALE_WARN   default 120 (secondi: oltre questo il JSON e' \"stale\")\n38|\n39|Uso:\n40|  python3 serve_dashboard.py                 # We need to understand why dashboard shows phantom data. The aggregator data shows bots with status "no_file". Likely the aggregator reads some per-bot state files from a directory like /home/sergio/denaro/health or node_data. Let's check what the aggregator actually is. There might be an aggregator service separate from exporter. Look for aggregator code: maybe in denaro2/gateway.py? Actually there is a gateway.py. Let's see.

Now we have the dashboard HTML and its JavaScript. It shows several sections:

- Hero equity: total equity (live) from total_equity.
- Equity breakdown per exchange from equity_breakdown.
- Bot cards: shows paper bots (flotta) from ORDER list (paper bots). It shows running status based on bots[n].status === 'running' and not stale.
- Grid sections: prices, balances, zabbix, system, docker.
- Later grid: node engine — tutti i bot (paper + live) from node_bots.
- Nodes denaro (3 macchine) from nodes.
- Servizi denaro per macchina from services.
- Trend equity (ultime 4h) from trend.
- Errori bot from node_errors.

Thus the dashboard shows both paper and live data depending on what the aggregator provides.

Now we need to answer the user's question: "ma la dashboard denaro.grivetto.eu/denaro mostra dati reali o solo fantasma e perché dannatissimo motivo con il progetto Money non stiamo guadagnando un tubo di nulla?"

We need to determine if the data shown is real or phantom. Based on our investigation:

- The aggregator's infra.json includes:
  - total_equity: real? It sums balances from exchange (OKX) plus maybe paper? Let's check the aggregator code to see how total_equity is computed.
  - bot_equity: from paper bots? Actually bot_equity is from the bots dict (which includes both live and paper bots). In the aggregator, bots dict includes both live and paper bots after merging.
  - equity_breakdown: per exchange (OKX, kraken) from exchange balances.
  - balances: from exchange balances.
  - prices: from ticker feed.
  - nodes: from node_totals (paper_running, live, equity, pnl, trades).
  - node_bots: from node_bots (includes both paper and live bots? Actually node_bots seems to be from the node's health files? Let's check aggregator code for node_bots.

Now we have the dashboard HTML and its JavaScript. It shows several sections:

- Hero equity: total equity (live) from total_equity.
- Equity breakdown per exchange from equity_breakdown.
- Bot cards: shows paper bots (flotta) from ORDER list (paper bots). It shows running status based on bots[n].status === 'running' and not stale.
- Grid sections: prices, balances, zabbix, system, docker.
- Later grid: node engine — tutti i bot (paper + live) from node_bots.
- Nodes denaro (3 macchine) from nodes.
- Servizi denaro per macchina from services.
- Trend equity (ultime 4h) from trend.
- Errori bot from node_errors.

Thus the dashboard shows both paper and live data depending on what the aggregator provides.

    risultato realizzato era circa -0,30 EUR;\n1058|    #  2) i bot con health vecchio (nodi spenti) contavano come \"running\".\n1059|    # Ora: \"running\" = vivo, non stale e recente; paper e live separati.\n1060|    live_running = [b for b in node_bots.values()\n1061|                    if b.get(\"status\") == \"running\" and not b.get(\"stale\")\n1062|                    and b.get(\"mode\") == \"live\"]\n1063|    paper_running = [b for b in node_bots.values()\n1064|                     if b.get(\"status\") == \"running\" and not b.get(\"stale\")\n1065|                     and b.get(\"mode\") != \"live\"]\n1066|    node_running = live_running\n1067|    data[\"node_total_pnl\"] = round(sum(b.get(\"pnl\", 0) for b in live_running), 4)\n1068|    data[\"node_paper_pnl\"] = round(sum(b.get(\"pnl\", 0) for b in paper_running), 4)\n1069|    data[\"node_live_bots\"] = len(live_running)\n1070|    data[\"node_paper_bots\"] = len(paper_running)\n1071|    data[\"node_stale_bots\"] = sorted(k for k, b in node_bots.items()\n1072|                                     if isinstance(b, dict) and b.get(\"stale\"))\n1073|    data[\"node_total_trades\"] = sum(b.get(\"trades\", 0) for b in live_running)\n1074|    wins = sum(b.get(\"wins\", 0) for b in live_running)\n1075|    losses = sum(b.get(\"losses\", 0) for b in live_running)\n1076|    data[\"node_win_rate\"] = round(wins / (wins + losses) * 100, 1) if (wins + losses) else 0\n1077|    data[\"node_errors\"] = {sym: b.get(\"error\", \"\")\n1078|                           for sym, b in node_bots.items() if b.get(\"error\")}\n1079|\n1080|    # 8b) Totali PER NODO: marcodg1 (locale) + nuvola + mc2 (via SSH)\n1081|    node_totals = {}\n1082|    all_node_names = [\"marcodg1\"] + list(REMOTE_NODES.keys())\n1083|    remote_prefixes = tuple(f\"{n}:\" for n in REMOTE_NODES)\n1084|    for node_name in all_node_names:\n1085|        if node_name == \"marcodg1\":\n1086|            nb = {k: v for k, v in node_bots.items()\n1087|                  if not k.startswith(remote_prefixes)}\n1088|        else:\n1089|            prefix = f\"{node_name}:\"\n1090|            nb = {k: v for k, v in node_bots.items() if k.startswith(prefix)}\n1091|        running = [b for b in nb.values()\n1092|                   if b.get(\"status\") == \"running\" and not b.get(\"stale\")\n1093|                   and b.get(\"mode\") == \"live\"]\n1094|        # paper: dedup per simbolo (lo stesso file puo' arrivare da due chiavi:\n1095|        # \"<nodo>:<SYM>\" dal glob legacy e \"<nodo>:paper:<SYM>\" dall'officina).\n1096|        _paper = {}\n1097|        for _bk, _bv in nb.items():\n1098|            if (_bv.get(\"status\") == \"running\" and not _bv.get(\"stale\")\n1099|                    and _bv.get(\"mode\") != \"live\"):\n1100|                _paper[_bv.get(\"symbol\") or _bk] = _bv\n1101|        paper_running = _paper\n1102|        node_totals[node_name] = {\n1103|            \"bots\": len(nb),\n1104|            \"running\": len(running),\n1105|            \"paper_running\": len(paper_running),\n1106|            \"pnl\": round(sum(b.get(\"pnl\", 0) for b in running), 4),\n1107|            \"trades\": sum(b.get(\"trades\", 0) for b in running),\n1108|            \"equity\": round(sum(b.get(\"total_equity\", 0) for b in running), 2),\n1109|            \"reachable\": (node_name == \"marcodg1\"\n1110|                          or any(h.get(\"timestamp\") for h in nb.values())),\n1111|        }\n1112|    data[\"node_totals\"] = node_totals\n1113|    data[\"canary\"] = collect_canary()\n1114|    data[\"services\"] = collect_services()\n1115|    data[\"trend\"] = read_trend()[-240:]\n1116|    return data\n1117|\n1118|\n1119|class Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):", "total_lines": 1174, "file_size": 49570, "truncated": true, "hint": "Use offset=1120 to continue reading (showing 900-1119 of 1174 lines)", "is_binary": false, "is_image": false, "not_found": false}

## 🤖 Hermes · 23:36
<details><summary>🧠 Ragionamento</summary>

Now we have a good understanding of the aggregator's output. Let's summarize:

- balances: real exchange balances (OKX main and subaccounts, via local .env or SSH). No Kraken.
- equity_breakdown: per-account equity in EUR (real_total, equity_detail from balance_eur).
- prices: live ticker prices from OKX via websocket feed (fetcher.py).
- nodes: reachability of each node (marcodg1, nuvola, mc2) via ping.
- node_bots: collected from each node's health files (via fetch_remote_node_bots). This includes both paper and live bots health JSON files (the ones we saw: btc_mc2.json, etc., and their _paper versions). The node_bots dict includes fields like total_equity, pnl, trades, mode (live/paper), status, timestamp, etc.
- bots: initially from live_bots_map (live health files). Then merged with node_bots to fill missing live bots (if live health file missing but node_bots has live data). Also includes remapping for symbol-only keys.
- Then staleness marking applied to bots dict.
- total_equity: if real_total > 0 (from balances), use that; else fallback to sum of bot equity (okx_eq + kraken_eq). In our case, real_total likely >0 because we have some balances (maybe small dust). Let's check actual balances.
- bot_equity: sum of total_equity from node_bots where status running and not stale, for okx and kraken separately.
- kraken_equity: similar but for trend-live nodes (maybe disabled).
- node_total_pnl: sum of pnl from live_running bots (mode live, status running, not stale).
- node_paper_pnl: sum of pnl from paper_running bots (mode != live).
- node_live_bots, node_paper_bots counts.
- node_total_trades, node_win_rate from live_running.
- node_errors: errors from node_bots.
- node_totals per node: includes running (live), paper_running, pnl, trades, equity (sum of total_equity from running bots), reachable.

Now, the dashboard's JavaScript renders:

- Hero equity: total_equity from data (line 418-419). So if real_total >0, it shows real equity from exchange balances; else shows sum of bot equity.
- Equity breakdown: from equity_breakdown (lines 420-431). It shows per-account equity if available, else OKX LIVE equity from bot_equity.
- Balances: from balances (lines 435-466). Shows each exchange label with total amounts.
- Prices: from prices (lines 444-452).
- Zabbix, system, docker: from respective sections.
- Flotta (paper bots): from bots dict (but filtered by ORDER list, which includes only paper bots). It shows running status based on bots[n].status === 'running' and not stale. Since bots dict includes both live and paper after merging, but ORDER only includes paper keys (e.g., 'mc2:paper:BTC/EUR'), so flotta shows paper bots only.
- Node engine — tutti i bot (paper + live): from node_bots (line 585-637). It shows each bot's status, equity, pnl, etc., but skips stale bots.
- Nodi denaro: from node_totals (lines 558-582). Shows per node: reachable, paper_running, live, equity (from live running bots), pnl, trades.
- Servizi denaro: from services (lines 534-555).
- Trend equity: from trend (lines 640-673).
- Errori bot: from node_errors (lines 675-681).

Thus the dashboard shows a mix of real data (balances, prices, node reachability, services) and derived data from bot health files (which may be paper or live). The bot health files we saw are paper bots (since they are *_paper.json). The live health files (btc_mc2.json etc.) do not exist (status no_file). Therefore, the live bots are not producing health files, meaning they are not running (or not writing health). The node_totals show live running count likely zero.

Let's verify node_totals from the infra.json we attempted to curl earlier but got truncated. Let's get the full infra.json.

## 🤖 Hermes · 23:36
<details><summary>🧠 Ragionamento</summary>

Now we have a good understanding of the aggregator's output. Let's summarize:

- balances: real exchange balances (OKX main and subaccounts, via local .env or SSH). No Kraken.
- equity_breakdown: per-account equity in EUR (real_total, equity_detail from balance_eur).
- prices: live ticker prices from OKX via websocket feed (fetcher.py).
- nodes: reachability of each node (marcodg1, nuvola, mc2) via ping.
- node_bots: collected from each node's health files (via fetch_remote_node_bots). This includes both paper and live bots health JSON files (the ones we saw: btc_mc2.json, etc., and their _paper versions). The node_bots dict includes fields like total_equity, pnl, trades, mode (live/paper), status, timestamp, etc.
- bots: initially from live_bots_map (live health files). Then merged with node_bots to fill missing live bots (if live health file missing but node_bots has live data). Also includes remapping for symbol-only keys.
- Then staleness marking applied to bots dict.
- total_equity: if real_total > 0 (from balances), use that; else fallback to sum of bot equity (okx_eq + kraken_eq). In our case, real_total likely >0 because we have some balances (maybe small dust). Let's check actual balances.
- bot_equity: sum of total_equity from node_bots where status running and not stale, for okx and kraken separately.
- kraken_equity: similar but for trend-live nodes (maybe disabled).
- node_total_pnl: sum of pnl from live_running bots (mode live, status running, not stale).
- node_paper_pnl: sum of pnl from paper_running bots (mode != live).
- node_live_bots, node_paper_bots counts.
- node_total_trades, node_win_rate from live_running.
- node_errors: errors from node_bots.
- node_totals per node: includes running (live), paper_running, pnl, trades, equity (sum of total_equity from running bots), reachable.

Now, the dashboard's JavaScript renders:

- Hero equity: total_equity from data (line 418-419). So if real_total >0, it shows real equity from exchange balances; else shows sum of bot equity.
- Equity breakdown: from equity_breakdown (lines 420-431). It shows per-account equity if available, else OKX LIVE equity from bot_equity.
- Balances: from balances (lines 435-466). Shows each exchange label with total amounts.
- Prices: from prices (lines 444-452).
- Zabbix, system, docker: from respective sections.
- Flotta (paper bots): from bots dict (but filtered by ORDER list, which includes only paper bots). It shows running status based on bots[n].status === 'running' and not stale. Since bots dict includes both live and paper after merging, but ORDER only includes paper keys (e.g., 'mc2:paper:BTC/EUR'), so flotta shows paper bots only.
- Node engine — tutti i bot (paper + live): from node_bots (line 585-637). It shows each bot's status, equity, pnl, etc., but skips stale bots.
- Nodi denaro: from node_totals (lines 558-582). Shows per node: reachable, paper_running, live, equity (from live running bots), pnl, trades.
- Servizi denaro: from services (lines 534-555).
- Trend equity: from trend (lines 640-673).
- Errori bot: from node_errors (lines 675-681).

Thus the dashboard shows a mix of real data (balances, prices, node reachability, services) and derived data from bot health files (which may be paper or live). The bot health files we saw are paper bots (since they are *_paper.json). The live health files (btc_mc2.json etc.) do not exist (status no_file). Therefore, the live bots are not producing health files, meaning they are not running (or not writing health). The node_totals show live running count likely zero.

Let's verify node_totals from the infra.json we attempted to curl earlier but got truncated. Let's get the full infra.json.

</details>

So total_equity is real (based on exchange balances). However, note that the USDC amount is large but likely not converted? The equity_breakdown only shows eur from balances (they converted using price?). The aggregator's balance_eur function likely converts each asset to EUR using prices from OKX ticker. So total_equity reflects real USD value of holdings.

- bot_equity: 0 (since no live bots running; all live bots status no_file). So bot_equity is zero.

- kraken_equity: 0 (maybe disabled).

- node_totals: shows each node: marcodg1 reachable true but bots 0, running 0, paper_running 0. nuvola reachable true, bots 6, running 0 (live), paper_running 6, pnl 0, trades 0, equity 0 (since equity sums from live running bots). mc2 reachable false, bots 0.

- services: shows which systemd services are active (1 = active, 0 = inactive). On marcodg1, only zabbix-agent is active (1). On nuvola, denaro-node-nuvola-trade (1), money-health-nuvola (1), zabbix-agent (1), zabbix-tunnel (1), denaro-exporter (1). On mc2, all zero.

Thus the dashboard shows:

- Hero equity: total_equity = 1098.29 EUR (real, from exchange balances).
- Equity breakdown: per account EUR (real, from balances).
- Balances: shows each exchange account with totals (real).
- Prices: live ticker from OKX (real).
- Nodes: reachability (real ping).
- Services: systemd status (real via SSH).
- Zabbix, docker, system: real via SSH.
- Flotta (paper bots): shows paper bots running (from bots dict, which are paper health files). So flotta shows paper bots status running (true) but they are paper, not live.
- Node engine — tutti i bot: shows node_bots (paper bots) with equity, pnl etc. All paper bots have zero pnl, zero trades, equity equal to capital (25 each except MINA which has slightly less due to drawdown). So they are not trading (no buys/sells). They are just sitting idle.
- Nodi denaro: shows per node: live running count (0), paper_running count (6 for nuvola), equity (from live running bots = 0), pnl (0), trades (0). So it shows zero live bots.
- Servizi denaro: shows which services are active (as above).
- Trend equity: shows historical equity from trend (maybe from some external source? The trend array shows equity values around 1098-1099, slowly varying; likely derived from total_equity over time? It seems to be a record of total_equity over time (maybe from a CSV). It shows small fluctuations but no significant growth.
- Errori bot: empty.

Thus the dashboard shows a mix of real data (balances, prices, node reachability, services) and simulated/paper data (paper bots). The live bots are not running (no health files). Therefore the dashboard is not showing live trading activity; it's showing paper bots (flotta) and node totals showing zero live bots. The equity shown is real (from exchange balances) but not increasing because no live trading is happening.

Now the user asks: "perché dannatissimo motivo con il progetto Money non stiamo guadagnando un tubo di nulla?" (why damn reason with the Money project we are not earning a damn thing?). The answer: Because live bots are not running (or not trading). The paper bots are idle (no trades). The system is not executing any live strategies; the live bots are configured but not producing health files, meaning they are either not started, crashed, or blocked by risk checks (e.g., min_notional, disabled symbols, missing keys). We need to check why live bots are not running.

We can examine the live bot configuration and logs. However, the user only asked to determine if dashboard data is real or phantom and why not earning. **Dashboard: dati reali vs fantasma**  
- **Reali**:  
  - `total_equity` = 1098,29 EUR (somma dei saldi OKX convertiti in EUR, visibile in `equity_breakdown` e `balances`).  
  - `prices` (ticker OKX in tempo reale).  
  - `nodes` → reachability (ping/Tailscale) e `services` → stato systemd (es. `denaro-node-nuvola-trade:1`, `zabbix-agent:1`).  
  - `zabbix`, `docker`, `system` → metriche raccolte via SSH (real‑time).  
- **Fantasma/simulato**:  
  - `bot_equity` = 0 (nessun bot live in esecuzione).  
  - Nella sezione **Flotta** compaiono solo i *paper bot* (mode = paper) con `status: running` ma senza ordini reali (sono simulati).  
  - Nella sezione **Nodi denaro** i campi `running` = 0, `paper_running` = 6 (nuvola) → nessun bot live attivo.  
  - `Errori bot` vuoto perché non ci sono processi live da monitorare.  

**Perché il progetto Money non guadagna nulla**  
1. **Live bot non avviati**: il servizio attivo su mc2 è `denaro-node-mc2.service`, che esegue la configurazione *paper* (`node_mc2_paper.yaml`). Non esiste alcun servizio live (es. `denaro-node-mc2-live.service`) in esecuzione.  
2. **Sottocapitalizzazione estrema**: nella configurazione live (`node_mc2.yaml`) ogni bot ha `capital: 0.015 EUR` mentre il `min_notional` è impostato a `1.0 EUR`. Con un capitale così basso nessun ordine può essere inviato all’exchange (l’ordine sarebbe sotto la soglia minima). Di conseguenza, anche se i bot live fossero avviati, non avrebbero operatività reale.  
3. I paper bot hanno `capital: 25.0 EUR` ciascuno (sufficiente per il `min_notional`), ma operano in modalità *paper* (nessun ordine reale), quindi non generano PnL reale.  

**Prossimo passo concreto (senza autorizzazione a operare reale)**  
1. **Verificare la mismatching di capitale/min_notional** (già fatto).  
2. **Proporre un aggiustamento del capitale live** basato sul rischio desiderato (es. 2 % del capitale totale per trade, con un minimo di 1,0 EUR per rispettare il `min_notional`). Con un equity di ~1100 EUR, un rischio del 2 % per trade dà ~22 EUR per bot; si potrebbero avviare 10‑15 bot con tale capitale oppure ridurre il numero di bot e aumentare il capitale per bot.  
3. **Prima di qualsiasi modifica**:  
   - Fare un backup dei file di configurazione live (`node_mc2.yaml`).  
   - Verificare che le chiavi API siano abilitate per il trading (controllare i file `.env` sotto `/home/sergio/denaro/secrets/` e `/home/sergio/money/.env`).  
   - Assicurarsi che il risk manager e il kill switch siano attivi (non disattivabili).  
4. **Dopo aver ottenuto l’autorizzazione esplicita dell’utente** (in quanto si tratta di avviare bot live che invieranno ordini reali):  
   - Aggiornare `node_mc2.yaml` con il nuovo capitale per bot (es. `capital: 20.0`).  
   - Riavviare il servizio live (creare/enable `denaro-node-mc2-live.service` o modificare quello esistente per puntare a `node_mc2.yaml`).  
   - Monitorare:  
     - Comparsa dei file di salute in `/home/sergio/denaro/health/` (es. `btc_mc2.json`).  
     - Presenza di ordini aperti o filled sull’exchange (chiamata di solo lettura a `/api/v5/account/positions` o equivalente).  
     - Incremento di `bot_equity` e `trades` nella dashboard.  
     - Andamento di `total_equity` nelle successive ore (deve reflettere il PnL reale dei bot live).  

**Come misurare il risultato**  
- Prima della modifica: registrare `total_equity`, `bot_equity`, `trades` (tutti zero per il live).  
- Dopo l’avvio live e un periodo di osservazione (es. 2‑4 h):  
  - `bot_equity` > 0 (indica che i bot hanno posizione reale).  
  - `trades` > 0 (numero di trade eseguiti).  
  - Variazione di `total_equity` coerente con la somma del PnL dei bot live (verificabile confrontando il delta di `total_equity` con la somma dei PnL riportati nei health file).  
  - Nessun aumento anomalo o perdite non spiegate (controllare i log dei bot per errori o attivazione del kill switch).  

Solo dopo aver constatato che i bot live stanno operando con segnali di trading reale e che il PnL è coerente con l’exchange si potrà considerare di procedere a ulteriori ottimizzazioni (es. affinamento della posizione sizing, leva, ecc.). Senza questi passi, qualsiasi dichiarazione di guadagno sarebbe non riconciliata e quindi vietata dal mandato.
